Your user needs these scopes assigned. [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/get-started#oauth).    - Scopes: `monitor:read_only`   **CLI**  `shell linode-cli monitor dashboards-view 1 `  [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/cloud-computing/docs/getting-started-with-the-linode-cli)
- [List supported service types](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/get-monitor-services.md): **Limited availability** Returns a paginated list of all current supported service types.  > 📘 > > This operation has a specific [rate limit](/reference/rate-limits#specific-operation-rate-limits).   **Permissions and scopes**  To call this operation, you need the following:  - **OAuth scopes**. Your user needs these scopes assigned. [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/get-started#oauth).    - Scopes: `monitor:read_only`   **CLI**  `shell linode-cli monitor service-list `  [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/cloud-computing/docs/getting-started-with-the-linode-cli)
- [Get details for a supported service type](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/get-monitor-services-for-service-type.md): **Limited availability** Returns details for a specific service type.  > 📘 > > - This operation has a specific [rate limit](/reference/rate-limits#specific-operation-rate-limits). > > - For more details on the metrics available for each service, see the [Metrics reference](https://techdocs.akamai.com/cloud-computing/docs/metrics-dimensions-parameters).   **Permissions and scopes**  To call this operation, you need the following:  - **OAuth scopes**. Your user needs these scopes assigned. [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/get-started#oauth).    - Scopes: `monitor:read_only`   **CLI**  `shell linode-cli monitor service-view dbaas `  [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/cloud-computing/docs/getting-started-with-the-linode-cli)
- [List dashboards for a service type](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/get-dashboards.md): **Limited availability** Returns all available dashboards for a given service type.  > 📘 > > This operation has a specific [rate limit](/reference/rate-limits#specific-operation-rate-limits).   **Permissions and scopes**  To call this operation, you need the following:  - **OAuth scopes**. Your user needs these scopes assigned. [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/get-started#oauth).    - Scopes: `monitor:read_only`   **CLI**  `shell linode-cli monitor dashboards-list dbaas `  [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/cloud-computing/docs/getting-started-with-the-linode-cli)
- [List metrics for a service type](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/get-monitor-information.md): **Limited availability** Returns metrics for a specific service type.  > 📘 > > - This operation has a specific [rate limit](/reference/rate-limits#specific-operation-rate-limits). > > - For more details on the metrics available for each service, see the [Metrics reference](https://techdocs.akamai.com/cloud-computing/docs/metrics-dimensions-parameters).   **Permissions and scopes**  To call this operation, you need the following:  - **OAuth scopes**. Your user needs these scopes assigned. [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/get-started#oauth).    - Scopes: `monitor:read_only`   **CLI**  `shell linode-cli monitor metrics-list dbaas `  [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/cloud-computing/docs/getting-started-with-the-linode-cli)
- [Create a token for a service type](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/post-get-token.md): **Limited availability** Returns a token that authenticates requests for the entities within a specific service type. To see your currently supported services, run the [List supported service types](/reference/get-monitor-services) operation. The token has a lifetime of six hours after you create it.  > 📘 > > When creating this token for Object Storage metrics, the token is accessible to all users on your account.  > 👍 There's a tutorial > > We offer an [example](/reference/get-started#authenticate-monitor-api-operations) you can follow to generate this token.   **Permissions and scopes**  To call this operation, you need the following:  - **OAuth scopes**. Your user needs these scopes assigned. [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/get-started#oauth).    - Scopes: `monitor:read_only`   **CLI**  `shell linode-cli monitor token-get dbaas \ --entity_ids 189690 \ --entity_ids 188020 --json `  [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/cloud-computing/docs/getting-started-with-the-linode-cli)
- [Get an entity's metrics](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/post-read-metric.md): **Limited availability** Returns metrics information for the individual entities within a specific service type. This operation also requires an `authorization: Bearer` [token](/reference/post-get-token) you've created for this `serviceType`.  > 📘 > > - This operation uses a different URL and version from standard Linode API operations. Verify you're using the URL with the `monitor-api.linode.com` hostname and include `v2` (or `v2beta`) as the version in the URL. > > - For more details on the metrics available for each service, see the [Metrics reference](https://techdocs.akamai.com/cloud-computing/docs/metrics-dimensions-parameters).
- [Create an alert definition](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/post-alert-definition-for-service-type.md): **Limited availability** Create a new alert definition for a specific service type. Akamai refers to these as `user` alerts. Run the [List supported service types](/reference/get-monitor-services) operation to see a list of services.   **Permissions and scopes**  To call this operation, you need the following:  - **OAuth scopes**. Your user needs these scopes assigned. [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/get-started#oauth).    - Scopes: `monitor:read_write`   **CLI**  `shell linode-cli alerts definition-create \ --channel_ids 546 \ --label CPU usage threshold maximum \ --rule_criteria.metric cpu_usage \ --rule_criteria.operator gt \ --rule_criteria.threshold 80 \ --severity 2 --trigger_conditions.evaluation_period_seconds 300 \ --trigger_conditions.polling_interval_seconds 300 \ --trigger_conditions.trigger_occurrences 3 \ `  [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/cloud-computing/docs/getting-started-with-the-linode-cli)
- [List alert definitions](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/get-alert-definitions.md): **Limited availability** Returns all available alert definitions on your account.  > 📘 > > [Filtering](/reference/filtering-and-sorting) is supported for specific objects, labeled as **Filterable**. However, only the `+and` and `+or` operators are supported, and you can't nest filter operators.   **Permissions and scopes**  To call this operation, you need the following:  - **OAuth scopes**. Your user needs these scopes assigned. [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/get-started#oauth).    - Scopes: `monitor:read_only`   **CLI**  `shell linode-cli alerts definitions-list-all `  [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/cloud-computing/docs/getting-started-with-the-linode-cli)
- [Get an alert definition](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/get-alert-definition.md): **Limited availability** Returns a specific alert definition.   **Permissions and scopes**  To call this operation, you need the following:  - **OAuth scopes**. Your user needs these scopes assigned. [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/get-started#oauth).    - Scopes: `monitor:read_only`   **CLI**  `shell linode-cli alerts definition-view dbaas 457 `  [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/cloud-computing/docs/getting-started-with-the-linode-cli)
- [Update an alert definition](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/put-alert-definition.md): **Limited availability** Update an existing alert definition. You need `read_only` access to the [OAuth scope](/reference/get-started#oauth-reference) for the selected `serviceType`. Only include the objects in the request that you want to update. Leave any object out of the request to leave it set as is.  When updating an alert definition you've [created](/reference/post-alert-definition-for-service-type), you can change the `status` to `enabled` or `disabled`. You can also modify the `label`, `description`, and `severity`. You can't update a `scope`, but you can update its applicable `entity_ids` or `regions`, as well as its `rule_criteria`, `trigger_conditions`, and `channel_ids` objects. If updating the `rule_criteria`, `channel_ids`, `entity_ids`, or `regions` fields, these points apply:  - If you want to keep an existing item, you need to include it in the list.  - If you want to remove an existing item, leave it out of the list.  - To add a new item, include it in the list.  - You can't pass an empty list to remove all items. This doesn't apply to the `entity_ids`, `regions`, or `dimension_filters` (in `rule_criteria`) list objects, because they are optional, while all other list objects are required.   **Permissions and scopes**  To call this operation, you need the following:  - **OAuth scopes**. Your user needs these scopes assigned. [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/get-started#oauth).    - Scopes: `monitor:read_write`   **CLI**  `shell linode-cli alerts definition-update dbaas 457 \ --status disabled \ --label Read-Write Channel (old) `  [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/cloud-computing/docs/getting-started-with-the-linode-cli)
- [Clone an alert definition](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/post-clone-alert-definition.md): **Limited availability** Clone an existing alert definition, for a specific service type. Run the [List alert definitions](/reference/get-alert-definitions) operation to see all of your available definitions. These points apply to cloning:  - You need to set a new `label` for the clone.  - You can clone your custom `user` alert definitions and preset Akamai `system` alert definition. Cloning a `system` alert definition generates a `user` alert definition.  - Leave out optional fields from the request to keep existing settings from the clone source. If you include a field that allows you to apply multiple values, you need to include any existing values to keep them. Run the [Get an alert definition](/reference/get-alert-definition) operation to see the current settings for your source. If you leave an existing value out, it's removed from the clone.  - You can't change the `scope` for a cloned alert definition:    - If the clone source `scope` is `region`, you can modify the `region` array to add or remove new regions. Leave this array out to create a new alert definition in the same regions.    - If the clone source `scope` is `entity`, you can add new `entity_ids` to include additional entities. To remove current entities, leave their `entity_id` out.   **Permissions and scopes**  To call this operation, you need the following:  - **OAuth scopes**. Your user needs these scopes assigned. [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/get-started#oauth).    - Scopes: `monitor:read_write`   **CLI**  `shell linode-cli alerts definition-clone dbaas 457 \ --label Read-Write Channel (clone) --description New alert definition cloned from Read-Write Channel --regions us-iad `  [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/cloud-computing/docs/getting-started-with-the-linode-cli)
- [List alert definitions for a service type](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/get-alert-definitions-for-service-type.md): **Limited availability** Returns all available alert definitions for a specific service type.   **Permissions and scopes**  To call this operation, you need the following:  - **OAuth scopes**. Your user needs these scopes assigned. [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/get-started#oauth).    - Scopes: `monitor:read_only`   **CLI**  `shell linode-cli alerts service-definitions-list dbaas `  [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/cloud-computing/docs/getting-started-with-the-linode-cli)
- [List entities assigned to an alert definition](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/get-alert-definition-entities.md): **Limited availability** Returns all entities for a specific alert definition used by a specific Akamai Cloud Computing service.   **Permissions and scopes**  To call this operation, you need the following:  - **OAuth scopes**. Your user needs these scopes assigned. [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/get-started#oauth).    - Scopes: `monitor:read_only`   **CLI**  `shell linode-cli alerts definition-entities-list dbaas 457 `  [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/cloud-computing/docs/getting-started-with-the-linode-cli)
- [Delete an alert definition](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/delete-alert-definition.md): **Limited availability** Delete a specific alert definition on your account.  > 📘 > > - You need `read_only` access to the [OAuth scope](/reference/get-started#oauth-reference) for the target `serviceType`. > > - An [alert definition](/reference/get-alert-definitions) with a `type` of `system` can't be deleted.   **Permissions and scopes**  To call this operation, you need the following:  - **OAuth scopes**. Your user needs these scopes assigned. [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/get-started#oauth).    - Scopes: `monitor:read_write`   **CLI**  `shell linode-cli alerts definition-delete dbaas 123 `  [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/cloud-computing/docs/getting-started-with-the-linode-cli)
- [Create a notification channel](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/post-notification-channel.md): **Limited availability** Create a new notification channel, to set up a communication method for a monitoring alert. This is referred to as a `user` channel and you can have a maximum of five on your account.   **Permissions and scopes**  To call this operation, you need the following:  - **OAuth scopes**. Your user needs these scopes assigned. [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/get-started#oauth).    - Scopes: `monitor:read_write`   **CLI**  `shell linode-cli alerts channels-create --label Email channel for Managed Database alerts \ --channel_type email \ --details.email.usernames johndoe \ --details.email.usernames janedoe `  [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/cloud-computing/docs/getting-started-with-the-linode-cli)
- [List notification channels](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/get-notification-channels.md): **Limited availability** Returns all of the monitoring alert notification channels available to your user. A notification channel is a method of communication used to send a monitoring alert.  > 📘 > > [Filtering](/reference/filtering-and-sorting) is supported for specific objects, labeled as **Filterable**. However, only the `+and` and `+or` operators are supported, and you can't nest filter operators.   **Permissions and scopes**  To call this operation, you need the following:  - **OAuth scopes**. Your user needs these scopes assigned. [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/get-started#oauth).    - Scopes: `monitor:read_only`   **CLI**  `shell linode-cli alerts channels-list `  [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/cloud-computing/docs/getting-started-with-the-linode-cli)
- [Get a notification channel](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/get-notification-channel.md): **Limited availability** Returns a specific monitoring alerts notification channel. A notification channel is the method of communication used to send a monitoring alert.   **Permissions and scopes**  To call this operation, you need the following:  - **OAuth scopes**. Your user needs these scopes assigned. [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/get-started#oauth).    - Scopes: `monitor:read_only`   **CLI**  `shell linode-cli alerts channels-view 754 `  [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/cloud-computing/docs/getting-started-with-the-linode-cli)
- [Update a notification channel](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/put-notification-channel.md): **Limited availability** Update an existing monitoring alert notification channel. A notification channel is the method of communication used to send a monitoring alert.  > 📘 > > - To maintain existing settings in a notification channel, you need to provide them when updating an object. For example, if you have a `channel_type` of `email` with `johndoe` and `janedoe` currently set as `usernames`, you need to include them if you're updating the `usernames` object with a new value. Run the [Get notification channel](/reference/get-notification-channel) operation and review the channel's existing settings. > > - You can't change the `channel_type` for an existing notification channel. > > - You can't update the default notification channel, or a `system` notification channel.   **Permissions and scopes**  To call this operation, you need the following:  - **OAuth scopes**. Your user needs these scopes assigned. [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/get-started#oauth).    - Scopes: `monitor:read_write`   **CLI**  `shell linode-cli alerts channels-update 754 --details.email.usernames johndoe / --details.email.usernames janedoe / --details.email.usernames bobdoe `  [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/cloud-computing/docs/getting-started-with-the-linode-cli)
- [List alerts for a notification channel](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/get-notification-channel-alerts.md): **Limited availability** Returns all monitoring alerts definitions that use a specific notification channel.   **Permissions and scopes**  To call this operation, you need the following:  - **OAuth scopes**. Your user needs these scopes assigned. [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/get-started#oauth).    - Scopes: `monitor:read_only`   **CLI**  `shell linode-cli alerts channels-alerts-list 754 `  [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/cloud-computing/docs/getting-started-with-the-linode-cli)
- [Delete a notification channel](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/delete-notification-channel.md): **Limited availability** Delete a specific monitoring alert notification channel with a `type` of `user`. You can't delete a `system` notification channel. You also can't delete a `user` channel that's actively used in an alert:  1. Run the [List alerts for a notification channel](/reference/get-notification-channel-alerts) operation, using the stored `channelId` for your notification channel.  2. Store the `id` for each alert that uses the notification channel.  3. Run the [Update an alert definition](/reference/put-alert-definition) operation for each stored alert `id`. Include the `channel_ids` object in the request and ensure that the notification `channelId` is _left out_. This removes the notification channel from the alert definition.   **Permissions and scopes**  To call this operation, you need the following:  - **OAuth scopes**. Your user needs these scopes assigned. [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/get-started#oauth).    - Scopes: `monitor:read_write`   **CLI**  `shell linode-cli alerts channels-delete 754 `  [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/cloud-computing/docs/getting-started-with-the-linode-cli)
- [Create a destination](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/post-destination.md): Create a new destination to serve as a sync point for logs data. You need a destination, before you can create a stream. This operation can only be accessed by account [users](/reference/get-users) with _unrestricted_ access. Talk to your local account administrator about access management.  > 🚧 > > By using this service, you acknowledge your obligations under the United States Department of Justice Bulk Sensitive Data Transaction Rule ("BSD Rule"). You also agree that you will not use the service to transfer, onward transfer, or otherwise make accessible any United States government-related data or bulk United States sensitive personal data to countries of concern or a covered person, as each of those terms and concepts are defined in the [BSD Rule](https://www.federalregister.gov/documents/2024/03/01/2024-04573/preventing-access-to-americans-bulk-sensitive-personal-data-and-united-states-government-related). Anyone using the service is solely responsible for compliance with the BSD Rule.  > 👍 There's a tutorial > > We offer an example API [workflow](/reference/configure-audit-log-delivery) you can follow to set up a stream and its destination.   **Permissions and scopes**  To call this operation, you need the following:  - **OAuth scopes**. Your user needs these scopes assigned. [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/get-started#oauth).    - Scopes: `monitor:read_write`   **CLI**  _CLI - Akamai Object Storage destination_  `shell linode-cli streams destination-create \ --label OBJ_logs_destination \ --type akamai_object_storage \ --details.host primary-bucket-1.us-east-12.linodeobjects.com \ --details.bucket_name primary-bucket \ --details.path audit-logs \ --details.access_key_id 1ABCD23EFG4HIJKLMNO5 \ --details.access_key_secret 1aB2CD3e4fgHi5JK6lmnop7qR8STU9VxYzabcdefHh `  _CLI - Custom HTTPS destination_  `shell linode-cli streams destination-create \ --label custom_https_logs_destination \ --type custom_https \ --details.endpoint_url https://my-site.com/log-storage/basicAuth \ --details.content_type application/json \ --details.data_compression gzip \ --details.authentication.type basic \ --details.authentication.details.basic_authentication_user John_Q \ --details.authentication.details.basic_authentication_password C0mPl3X_p@$$w0Rd `  [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/cloud-computing/docs/getting-started-with-the-linode-cli)
- [List destinations](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/get-destinations.md): Returns all available logs destinations. Destinations are used as sync points for log data. This operation can only be accessed by account [users](/reference/get-users) with _unrestricted_ access. Talk to your local account administrator about access management.   **Permissions and scopes**  To call this operation, you need the following:  - **OAuth scopes**. Your user needs these scopes assigned. - [Get a destination](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/get-destination.md): Returns a specific logs destination. This operation can only be accessed by account [users](/reference/get-users) with _unrestricted_ access. Talk to your local account administrator about access management.   **Permissions and scopes**  To call this operation, you need the following:  - **OAuth scopes**. Your user needs these scopes assigned. [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/get-started#oauth).    - Scopes: `monitor:read_only`   **CLI**  `shell linode-cli streams destination-view 456 `  [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/cloud-computing/docs/getting-started-with-the-linode-cli)
- [Update a destination](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/put-destination.md): Update an existing logs destination. Include the appropriate `destinationId` as a path parameter. This operation can only be accessed by account [users](/reference/get-users) with _unrestricted_ access. Talk to your local account administrator about access management.  > 🚧 > > By using this service, you acknowledge your obligations under the United States Department of Justice Bulk Sensitive Data Transaction Rule ("BSD Rule"). You also agree that you will not use the service to transfer, onward transfer, or otherwise make accessible any United States government-related data or bulk United States sensitive personal data to countries of concern or a covered person, as each of those terms and concepts are defined in the [BSD Rule](https://www.federalregister.gov/documents/2024/03/01/2024-04573/preventing-access-to-americans-bulk-sensitive-personal-data-and-united-states-government-related). Anyone using the service is solely responsible for compliance with the BSD Rule.   **Permissions and scopes**  To call this operation, you need the following:  - **OAuth scopes**. Your user needs these scopes assigned. [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/get-started#oauth).    - Scopes: `monitor:read_write`   **CLI**  `shell linode-cli streams destination-update 456  \ --label New destination for Managed Database logs \ --details.host secondary-bucket-1.us-east-12.linodeobjects.com \ --details.bucket_name secondary-bucket \ --details.region us-east \ --details.path audit-logs \ --details.access_key_id 456 `  [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/cloud-computing/docs/getting-started-with-the-linode-cli)
- [Get a destination's history](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/get-destination-history.md): Returns all versions of a specific logs destination. Versions are listed in ascending order. This operation can only be accessed by account [users](/reference/get-users) with _unrestricted_ access. Talk to your local account administrator about access management.   **Permissions and scopes**  To call this operation, you need the following:  - **OAuth scopes**. Your user needs these scopes assigned. [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/get-started#oauth).    - Scopes: `monitor:read_only`   **CLI**  `shell linode-cli streams destination-history-view 456 `  [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/cloud-computing/docs/getting-started-with-the-linode-cli)
- [Delete a destination](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/delete-destination.md): Delete a specific logs destination. Include the appropriate `destinationId` as a path parameter. This operation can only be accessed by account [users](/reference/get-users) with _unrestricted_ access. Talk to your local account administrator about access management. You can't delete a destination that's part of an active logs stream:  1. With your target `destinationId` stored, run the [List streams](/reference/get-stream) operation and store the `id` for all streams that are using the destination.  2. Run the [Update a stream](/reference/put-stream) operation for each stored stream `id`, and remove the target destination.   **Permissions and scopes**  To call this operation, you need the following:  - **OAuth scopes**. Your user needs these scopes assigned. [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/get-started#oauth).    - Scopes: `monitor:read_write`   **CLI**  `shell linode-cli streams destination-delete 456 `  [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/cloud-computing/docs/getting-started-with-the-linode-cli)
- [Create a stream](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/post-stream.md): Create a new stream to track logs for an Akamai Cloud Computing service. Consider these points before creating a stream:  - You first need to [create the destination](/reference/post-destination) where you want to store log data.  - By default, you can only set up a single stream for each audit log type. Talk to your account team if you need additional streams.  - This operation can only be accessed by account [users](/reference/get-users) with _unrestricted_ access. Talk to your local account administrator about access management.  > 🚧 > > By using this service, you acknowledge your obligations under the United States Department of Justice Bulk Sensitive Data Transaction Rule ("BSD Rule"). You also agree that you will not use the service to transfer, onward transfer, or otherwise make accessible any United States government-related data or bulk United States sensitive personal data to countries of concern or a covered person, as each of those terms and concepts are defined in the [BSD Rule](https://www.federalregister.gov/documents/2024/03/01/2024-04573/preventing-access-to-americans-bulk-sensitive-personal-data-and-united-states-government-related). Anyone using the service is solely responsible for compliance with the BSD Rule.  > 👍 There's a tutorial > > We offer an example API [workflow](/reference/config-audit-log-delivery) you can follow to set up a stream and its destination.   **Permissions and scopes**  To call this operation, you need the following:  - **OAuth scopes**. Your user needs these scopes assigned. [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/get-started#oauth).    - Scopes: `monitor:read_write`   **CLI**  `shell linode-cli streams create \ --label Logs stream for Managed Databases \ --type audit_logs \ --destinations 12345 \ `  [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/cloud-computing/docs/getting-started-with-the-linode-cli)
- [List streams](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/get-streams.md): Returns all available streams used for log-tracking with your Akamai Cloud Computing services. This operation can only be accessed by account [users](/reference/get-users) with _unrestricted_ access. Talk to your local account administrator about access management.   **Permissions and scopes**  To call this operation, you need the following:  - **OAuth scopes**. Your user needs these scopes assigned. [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/get-started#oauth).    - Scopes: `monitor:read_only`   **CLI**  `shell linode-cli streams list `  [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/cloud-computing/docs/getting-started-with-the-linode-cli)
- [Get a stream](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/get-stream.md): Returns a specific logs stream. Include the appropriate `streamId` as a path parameter. Run the [List streams](/reference/get-streams) operation and store the `id` for the applicable one. This operation can only be accessed by account [users](/reference/get-users) with _unrestricted_ access. - [Update a stream](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/put-stream.md): Update an existing logs stream. You can't update a stream's `type`. This operation can only be accessed by account [users](/reference/get-users) with _unrestricted_ access. Talk to your local account administrator about access management.  > 🚧 > > By using this service, you acknowledge your obligations under the United States Department of Justice Bulk Sensitive Data Transaction Rule ("BSD Rule"). You also agree that you will not use the service to transfer, onward transfer, or otherwise make accessible any United States government-related data or bulk United States sensitive personal data to countries of concern or a covered person, as each of those terms and concepts are defined in the [BSD Rule](https://www.federalregister.gov/documents/2024/03/01/2024-04573/preventing-access-to-americans-bulk-sensitive-personal-data-and-united-states-government-related). Anyone using the service is solely responsible for compliance with the BSD Rule.   **Permissions and scopes**  To call this operation, you need the following:  - **OAuth scopes**. Your user needs these scopes assigned. [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/get-started#oauth).    - Scopes: `monitor:read_write`   **CLI**  `shell linode-cli streams update 890 \ --label Logs stream for Managed Databases \ --status inactive \ --destinations 12345 `  [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/cloud-computing/docs/getting-started-with-the-linode-cli)
- [Get a stream's history](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/get-stream-history.md): Returns all versions of a specific logs stream. ## API Reference: Network transfer prices

- [Network transfer prices index](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/network-transfer-prices/llms.txt): full category index
- [List network transfer prices](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/get-network-transfer-prices.md): Returns collection of network transfer prices, including any region-specific rates.   **Permissions and scopes**  To call this operation, you need the following:  - **Identity and access permissions**. This operation is internet-facing. No permissions are required.   **CLI**  `shell linode-cli network-transfer prices `  [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/cloud-computing/docs/getting-started-with-the-linode-cli)

## API Reference: Networking

- [Networking index](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/networking/llms.txt): full category index
- [Create a firewall](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/post-firewalls.md): Creates a Firewall to filter network traffic.  - Use `rules` to create inbound and outbound access rules. Rule versions increment from `1` whenever the firewall's `rules` change.  - Use `devices` to assign a firewall to a service such as a Linode that is using legacy config profiles, a public Linode interface, a regular VPC Linode interface, or a NodeBalancer. The firewall’s rules are then applied to that service.    - For Linodes using Linode interfaces, firewalls need to be assigned to `interfaces` and not the `linodes`. Firewall templates are available for both regular VPC Linode interfaces and public Linode interfaces, and come with pre-configured protection rules.    - For Linodes using legacy configuration profiles, firewalls are applied through the Linode. Public and VPC interfaces are subject to the firewall rules, while VLAN interfaces are not.  - Currently, firewalls can be assigned to Linodes with legacy configuration profiles, public or regular VPC Linode interfaces, and NodeBalancers.      - The same firewall can be assigned to multiple services at a time.  - Use `firewall_id` to assign a firewall when [creating a Linode](/reference/post-linode-instance) or when [adding a Linode interface](/reference/post-linode-interface).  - A service can have one assigned firewall enabled at a time.  - Assigned Linodes must not have any ongoing live migrations.  - A `firewall_create` event is generated when this operation succeeds.   **Permissions and scopes**  To call this operation, you need the following:  - **Identity and access permissions**. Your user needs a role with these permissions assigned. [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/cloud-computing/docs/identity-access-cm-available-roles).   - Permissions: `create_firewall` - **OAuth scopes**. Your user needs these scopes assigned. [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/get-started#oauth).    - Scopes: `firewall:read_write`   **CLI**  `shell linode-cli firewalls create \   --label example-firewall \   --rules.outbound_policy ACCEPT \   --rules.inbound_policy DROP \   --rules.inbound '[{"protocol": "TCP", "ports": "22, 80, 8080, 443", "addresses": {"ipv4": ["192.0.2.0/24", "198.51.100.2/32"], "ipv6": ["2001:DB8::/128"]}, "action": "ACCEPT"}]' \   --rules.outbound '[{"protocol": "TCP", "ports": "49152-65535", "addresses": {"ipv4": ["192.0.2.0/24", "198.51.100.2/32"],"ipv6": ["2001:DB8::/128"]}, "action": "DROP", "label": "outbound-rule123", "description": "An example outbound rule description."}]' `  [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/cloud-computing/docs/getting-started-with-the-linode-cli)
- [List firewalls](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/get-firewalls.md): Returns a paginated list of accessible Firewalls.   **Permissions and scopes**  To call this operation, you need the following:  - **Identity and access permissions**. Your user needs a role with these permissions assigned. [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/cloud-computing/docs/identity-access-cm-available-roles).   - Permissions: `view_firewall` - **OAuth scopes**. Your user needs these scopes assigned. [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/get-started#oauth).    - Scopes: `firewall:read_only`   **CLI**  `shell linode-cli firewalls list `  [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/cloud-computing/docs/getting-started-with-the-linode-cli)
- [Get a firewall](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/get-firewall.md): Get a specific Firewall resource by its ID. The Firewall's Devices will not be returned in the response. Instead, run the [List firewall devices](/reference/get-firewall-devices) operation to review them.   **Permissions and scopes**  To call this operation, you need the following:  - **Identity and access permissions**. Your user needs a role with these permissions assigned. [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/cloud-computing/docs/identity-access-cm-available-roles).   - Permissions: `view_firewall` - **OAuth scopes**. Your user needs these scopes assigned. [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/get-started#oauth).    - Scopes: `firewall:read_only`   **CLI**  `shell linode-cli firewalls view 123 `  [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/cloud-computing/docs/getting-started-with-the-linode-cli)
- [Update a firewall](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/put-firewall.md): Updates information for a firewall.  - Assigned Linodes must not have any ongoing live migrations.  - If this operation changes a firewall's status, it generates a corresponding `firewall_enable` or `firewall_disable` event.  This operation doesn't affect some parts of a firewall's configuration:  - This operation doesn't set a firewall's devices. Instead, run the [Create a firewall device](/reference/post-firewall-device) and [Delete a firewall device](/reference/delete-firewall-device) operations to assign and remove this firewall from services.  - A firewall's rules can't be changed with this operation. Instead, run the [Update firewall rules](/reference/put-firewall-rules) operation to update your rules.  - Use this operation to set a firewall's status to `enabled` or `disabled`.  But to set it to `deleted`, run the [Delete a firewall](/reference/delete-firewall) operation.  - An assigned default firewall can't be disabled.   **Permissions and scopes**  To call this operation, you need the following:  - **Identity and access permissions**. Your user needs a role with these permissions assigned. [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/cloud-computing/docs/identity-access-cm-available-roles).   - Permissions: `update_firewall` - **OAuth scopes**. Your user needs these scopes assigned. [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/get-started#oauth).    - Scopes: `firewall:read_write`   **CLI**  `shell linode-cli firewalls update 123 \   --status disabled `  [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/cloud-computing/docs/getting-started-with-the-linode-cli)
- [Delete a firewall](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/delete-firewall.md): Delete a firewall. This also removes all of the firewall's rules from any services the firewall was assigned to.  - Assigned Linodes must not have any ongoing live migrations.  - A `firewall_delete` event is generated when this operation returns successfully.  - An assigned default firewall can't be deleted.   **Permissions and scopes**  To call this operation, you need the following:  - **Identity and access permissions**. Your user needs a role with these permissions assigned. [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/cloud-computing/docs/identity-access-cm-available-roles).   - Permissions: `delete_firewall` - **OAuth scopes**. Your user needs these scopes assigned. [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/get-started#oauth).    - Scopes: `firewall:read_write`   **CLI**  `shell linode-cli firewalls delete 123 `  [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/cloud-computing/docs/getting-started-with-the-linode-cli)
- [List firewall rule versions](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/get-firewall-rule-versions.md): Lists the current and historical rules of the firewall (that is not deleted), using `version`. Whenever rules update, the `version` increments from `1`.   **Permissions and scopes**  To call this operation, you need the following:  - **Identity and access permissions**. Your user needs a role with these permissions assigned. [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/cloud-computing/docs/identity-access-cm-available-roles).   - Permissions: `list_firewall_rule_versions` - **OAuth scopes**. Your user needs these scopes assigned. [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/get-started#oauth).    - Scopes: `firewall:read_only`   **CLI**  `shell linode-cli firewalls versions-list 123 `  [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/cloud-computing/docs/getting-started-with-the-linode-cli)
- [Get a firewall rule version](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/get-firewall-rule-version.md): Get a specific firewall rule version for an `enabled` or `disabled` firewall.   **Permissions and scopes**  To call this operation, you need the following:  - **OAuth scopes**. Your user needs these scopes assigned. [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/get-started#oauth).    - Scopes: `firewall:read_only`   **CLI**  `shell linode-cli firewalls version-view \   123 2 `  [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/cloud-computing/docs/getting-started-with-the-linode-cli)
- [List firewall rules](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/get-firewall-rules.md): Returns the inbound and outbound Rules for a Firewall.   **Permissions and scopes**  To call this operation, you need the following:  - **Identity and access permissions**. Your user needs a role with these permissions assigned. [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/cloud-computing/docs/identity-access-cm-available-roles).   - Permissions: `list_firewall_rules` - **OAuth scopes**. Your user needs these scopes assigned. [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/get-started#oauth).    - Scopes: `firewall:read_only`   **CLI**  `shell linode-cli firewalls rules-list 123 `  [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/cloud-computing/docs/getting-started-with-the-linode-cli)
- [Update firewall rules](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/put-firewall-rules.md): Updates the inbound and outbound Rules for a Firewall.  - Assigned Linodes must not have any ongoing live migrations.  - **Note**. This operation replaces all of a Firewall's `inbound` and `outbound` rulesets with the values specified in your request.   **Permissions and scopes**  To call this operation, you need the following:  - **Identity and access permissions**. Your user needs a role with these permissions assigned. [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/cloud-computing/docs/identity-access-cm-available-roles).   - Permissions: `update_firewall_rules` - **OAuth scopes**. Your user needs these scopes assigned. [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/get-started#oauth).    - Scopes: `firewall:read_write`   **CLI**  ``shell linode-cli firewalls rules-update 123 \   --inbound '[{"action":"ACCEPT", "protocol": "TCP", "ports": "22, 80, 8080, 443", "addresses": {"ipv4": ["192.0.2.0/24", "198.51.100.2/32"], "ipv6": ["2001:DB8::/128"]}}]' \   --outbound '[{"action":"DROP","protocol": "TCP", "ports": "49152-65535", "addresses": {"ipv4": ["192.0.2.0/24", "198.51.100.2/32"], "ipv6": ["2001:DB8::/128`"]}}]' ``  [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/cloud-computing/docs/getting-started-with-the-linode-cli)
- [List default firewalls](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/get-firewall-settings.md): Returns default firewalls for Linodes, Linode VPC and public interfaces, and NodeBalancers.   **Permissions and scopes**  To call this operation, you need the following:  - **Identity and access permissions**. Your user needs a role with these permissions assigned. [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/cloud-computing/docs/identity-access-cm-available-roles).   - Permissions: `list_default_firewalls` - **OAuth scopes**. Your user needs these scopes assigned. [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/get-started#oauth).    - Scopes: `firewall:read_only`   **CLI**  `shell linode-cli firewalls firewall-settings-list `  [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/cloud-computing/docs/getting-started-with-the-linode-cli)
- [Update default firewalls](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/put-firewall-settings.md): You can update or add a default firewall to:  - Linodes using legacy config profile interfaces  - Linode VPC interfaces and Linode public interfaces  - NodeBalancers  If a firewall isn't provided during service creation, a default firewall is assigned, unless you have opted out of firewall protection.  > 📘 > > Default firewalls on Linodes with Linode interfaces are applied to the interfaces, not the Linode itself. > > Default firewalls on Linodes with legacy configuration profile interfaces are applied directly to the Linode, not its interfaces.   **Permissions and scopes**  To call this operation, you need the following:  - **Identity and access permissions**. Your user needs a role with these permissions assigned. [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/cloud-computing/docs/identity-access-cm-available-roles).   - Permissions: `update_default_firewalls` - **OAuth scopes**. Your user needs these scopes assigned. [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/get-started#oauth).    - Scopes: `account:read_write`   **CLI**  `shell linode-cli firewalls firewall-settings-update \   --default_firewall_ids.vpc_interface 1 \   --default_firewall_ids.public_interface 2 \   --default_firewall_ids.nodebalancer 3 \   --default_firewall_ids.linode 5 `  [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/cloud-computing/docs/getting-started-with-the-linode-cli)
- [List firewall templates](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/get-firewall-templates.md): Returns a paginated list of a firewall templates. There are firewall templates specifically for VPC interfaces and public interfaces. Firewall templates come with some protection rules already configured.   **Permissions and scopes**  To call this operation, you need the following:  - **Identity and access permissions**. This operation is internet-facing. No permissions are required. - **OAuth scopes**. Your user needs these scopes assigned. [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/get-started#oauth).    - Scopes: `firewall:read_only`   **CLI**  `shell linode-cli firewalls templates-list `  [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/cloud-computing/docs/getting-started-with-the-linode-cli)
- [Get a firewall template](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/get-firewall-template.md): Gets a `vpc` or `public` firewall template you can use with Linode VPC and public interfaces. Firewall templates come with some protection rules already configured.  The public interface's firewall template allows for login with SSH, and regular networking control data. You should further strengthen these rules by limiting the allowed IPv4 and IPv6 ranges.  The VPC interface's firewall template allows for login with SSH, regular networking control data, and inbound traffic from the VPC address space. You should further strengthen these rules by limiting the allowed IPv4 and IPv6 ranges.   **Permissions and scopes**  To call this operation, you need the following:  - **Identity and access permissions**. This operation is internet-facing. No permissions are required. - **OAuth scopes**. Your user needs these scopes assigned. [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/get-started#oauth).    - Scopes: `firewall:read_only`   **CLI**  `shell linode-cli firewalls template-view vpc `  [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/cloud-computing/docs/getting-started-with-the-linode-cli)
- [Create a firewall device](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/post-firewall-device.md): Creates a firewall device, which assigns a firewall to a service (referred to as the device's `entity`) and applies the firewall's rules to the device.  - Currently, devices with `linode`, `linode_interface`, and `nodebalancer` entity types are accepted.   - The `linode` type is not allowed for Linodes using Linode interfaces.   - The `linode_interface` type is not allowed for legacy config interfaces. For VPC and public legacy config profile interfaces, the firewall is applied through the `linode` device.   - The `linode_interface` type only supports regular Linode interfaces, (public or VPC Linode interfaces).  - Firewalls only apply to inbound TCP traffic to NodeBalancers.  - A firewall can be assigned to multiple services at a time.  - A service can have one assigned firewall at a time.  - Assigned Linodes must not have any ongoing live migrations.  - A `firewall_device_add` event is generated when the firewall device is added successfully.   **Permissions and scopes**  To call this operation, you need the following:  - **Identity and access permissions**. Your user needs a role with these permissions assigned. [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/cloud-computing/docs/identity-access-cm-available-roles).   - Permissions: `create_firewall_device` - **OAuth scopes**. Your user needs these scopes assigned. [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/get-started#oauth).    - Scopes: `firewall:read_write`   **CLI**  `shell linode-cli firewalls device-create 123 \   --id 456 \   --type "linode" `  [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/cloud-computing/docs/getting-started-with-the-linode-cli)
- [List firewall devices](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/get-firewall-devices.md): Returns a paginated list of a firewall's devices. A firewall device assigns a firewall to a service (referred to as the device's `entity`).   **Permissions and scopes**  To call this operation, you need the following:  - **Identity and access permissions**. Your user needs a role with these permissions assigned. [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/cloud-computing/docs/identity-access-cm-available-roles).   - Permissions: `view_firewall` - **OAuth scopes**. Your user needs these scopes assigned. [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/get-started#oauth).    - Scopes: `firewall:read_only`   **CLI**  `shell linode-cli firewalls devices-list 123 `  [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/cloud-computing/docs/getting-started-with-the-linode-cli)
- [Get a firewall device](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/get-firewall-device.md): Returns information for a Firewall Device, which assigns a Firewall to a service (referred to as the Device's `entity`).   **Permissions and scopes**  To call this operation, you need the following:  - **Identity and access permissions**. Your user needs a role with these permissions assigned. [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/cloud-computing/docs/identity-access-cm-available-roles).   - Permissions: `view_firewall_device` - **OAuth scopes**. Your user needs these scopes assigned. [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/get-started#oauth).    - Scopes: `firewall:read_only`   **CLI**  `shell linode-cli firewalls device-view \   123 456 `  [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/cloud-computing/docs/getting-started-with-the-linode-cli)
- [Delete a firewall device](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/delete-firewall-device.md): Removes a Firewall Device, which removes a Firewall from the service it was assigned to by the Device. This removes all of the Firewall's Rules from the service. If any other Firewalls have been assigned to the service, then those Rules remain in effect.  - Assigned Linodes must not have any ongoing live migrations.  - A `firewall_device_remove` Event is generated when the Firewall Device is removed successfully.   **Permissions and scopes**  To call this operation, you need the following:  - **Identity and access permissions**. Your user needs a role with these permissions assigned. [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/cloud-computing/docs/identity-access-cm-available-roles).   - Permissions: `delete_firewall_device` - **OAuth scopes**. Your user needs these scopes assigned. [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/get-started#oauth).    - Scopes: `firewall:read_write`   **CLI**  `shell linode-cli firewalls device-delete 123 456 `  [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/cloud-computing/docs/getting-started-with-the-linode-cli)
- [Allocate an IP address](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/post-allocate-ip.md): To manage your network configuration, you can allocate ephemeral or reserved IP addresses depending on your need for temporary or permanent connectivity. The Linode must be configured to support additional addresses. Before you allocate, [open a support ticket](/reference/post-ticket) to request additional addresses.  Ephemeral IPv4 addresses are temporary and tied directly to a specific resource rather than your account. They're typically recycled and returned to the pool once the associated resource is deleted.  Reserved IPs are static, permanent addresses ideal for services requiring a consistent entry point. A reserved IP is permanently locked to the specific region where it was created. You can create a reserved IP independently of a Linode, allowing it to exist on your account with or without an active assignment, but it can only be assigned to resources within that same region. Charges apply as soon as a reserved IP is added to your account, regardless of whether it's assigned to a resource.  > 📘 > > You can run this operation for Linodes with legacy configuration interfaces. You can't use it for Linodes with Linode interfaces. To allocate an IP for a Linode with Linode interfaces, use the [Add a Linode interface](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/post-linode-interface) operation.   **Permissions and scopes**  To call this operation, you need the following:  - **OAuth scopes**. Your user needs these scopes assigned. [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/get-started#oauth).    - Scopes: `ips:read_write`   **CLI**  _CLI example - Ephemeral IP_  `shell linode-cli networking ip-add \   --type ipv4 \   --public false \   --linode_id 123 `  _CLI example - Reserved IP_  `shell linode-cli networking ip-add \   --type ipv4 \   --public true \   --linode_id 123 \   --reserved true \   --region us-east `  [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/cloud-computing/docs/getting-started-with-the-linode-cli)
- [List IP addresses](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/get-ips.md): Returns a paginated list of public IP addresses associated with your account. Private addresses are excluded.  > 👍 > > if your application frequently accesses this operation and doesn't require IPv6 RDNS data, you can use the `skip_ipv6_rdns` query string to improve performance.   **Permissions and scopes**  To call this operation, you need the following:  - **OAuth scopes**. Your user needs these scopes assigned. [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/get-started#oauth).    - Scopes: `ips:read_only`   **CLI**  `shell linode-cli networking ips-list `  [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/cloud-computing/docs/getting-started-with-the-linode-cli)
- [Assign IP addresses](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/post-assign-ips.md): Assigns a set of IPv4 addresses and IPv6 ranges to Linodes within a single region. This operation lets you swap, shuffle, or reorganize IP addresses among your Linodes, and assign reserved IPs that aren't currently assigned to specific Linode instances.  The following restrictions apply:  - All addresses must be an IPv4 address or an IPv6 range you can access in the specified region. This includes unassigned reserved IP addresses belonging to your account. - If you specify a reserved IP, it must be reserved in your account. Assigning an unassigned reserved IP moves the IP from your account's general pool and becomes an assigned resource tied to a specific Linode interface. Using a reserved IP that you don't own results in a 400 error. - You can't swap an assigned reserved IP with a newly created ephemeral IP using this operation. To convert or toggle the status of an IP, use the [Update an IP address's RDNS and reserved setting](/reference/put-ip) operation. - All Linodes need to have at least one public IPv4 address assigned. - Linodes that use Linode interfaces can't be assigned a private IPv4 address. - Linodes that use configuration profile interfaces may have a maximum of one assigned private IPv4 address. - Linodes are restricted to one assigned IPv6 range. - Shared IP addresses can't be swapped between Linodes.  [Open a support ticket](/reference/post-ticket) to request additional IPv4 addresses or IPv6 ranges beyond standard account limits.  > 📘 > > Removing an IP address that has been set as a Managed Linode's `ssh.ip` causes the Managed Linode's SSH access settings to reset to their default values.  To view and configure Managed Linode SSH settings, use the following operations:  - [Get a Linode's managed settings](/reference/get-managed-linode-setting) - [Update a Linode's managed settings](/reference/put-managed-linode-setting)   **Permissions and scopes**  To call this operation, you need the following:  - **OAuth scopes**. Your user needs these scopes assigned. [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/get-started#oauth).    - Scopes: `ips:read_write, linodes:read_write`   **CLI**  `shell linode-cli networking ip-assign \   --region us-east \   --assignments.address 192.0.2.1 \   --assignments.linode_id 123 \   --assignments.address 2001:db8:3c4d:15::/64 \   --assignments.linode_id 234 `  [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/cloud-computing/docs/getting-started-with-the-linode-cli)
- [Share IP addresses](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/post-share-ips.md): Configure shared IPs.  IP sharing allows IP address reassignment (also referred to as IP failover) from one Linode to another if the primary Linode becomes unresponsive. This means that requests to the primary Linode's IP address can be automatically rerouted to secondary Linodes at the configured shared IP addresses.  IP failover requires configuration of a [BGP based failover service](https://techdocs.akamai.com/cloud-computing/docs/configure-failover-on-a-compute-instance) within the internal system of the primary Linode.  > 📘 > > A public IPv4 address can't be shared if it's configured for a 1:1 NAT on a legacy configuration profile VPC interface or on a Linode VPC interface.   **Permissions and scopes**  To call this operation, you need the following:  - **OAuth scopes**. Your user needs these scopes assigned. [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/get-started#oauth).    - Scopes: `ips:read_write, linodes:read_write`   **CLI**  `shell linode-cli networking ip-share \   --linode_id 123 \   --ips 192.0.2.1 \   --ips 2001:db8:3c4d:15:: `  [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/cloud-computing/docs/getting-started-with-the-linode-cli)
- [Get an IP address](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/get-ip.md): Returns information about a single IP Address on your Account.   **Permissions and scopes**  To call this operation, you need the following:  - **OAuth scopes**. Your user needs these scopes assigned. [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/get-started#oauth).    - Scopes: `ips:read_only`   **CLI**  `shell linode-cli networking ip-view 97.107.143.141 `  [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/cloud-computing/docs/getting-started-with-the-linode-cli)
- [Update an IP address](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/put-ip.md): Updates the configuration of an existing IP address. Use this operation to set Reverse DNS (RDNS) and to toggle the `reserved` status of an IPv4 address. You can update both the `reserved` status and the `rdns` field in a single request, if the IP is assigned to a Linode.  **RDNS**: - Forward DNS must already be set up for reverse DNS to be applied. - If you set the `rdns` to `null` for public IPv4 addresses, it resets to the default `ip.linodeusercontent.com` RDNS value. - You can't set RDNS on an unassigned reserved IP. The IP must be assigned to a Linode to update its RDNS record.  **Reserved IP**: - Setting `reserved` to `true` on an ephemeral IP converts it to a reserved IP. - Setting `reserved` to `false` on a reserved IP converts it to an ephemeral IP. If you convert an unassigned reserved IP to ephemeral, the IP is immediately released from your account. Unreserving this IP removes it from your reserved list and makes it unavailable to assign. This action can't be undone.   **Permissions and scopes**  To call this operation, you need the following:  - **OAuth scopes**. Your user needs these scopes assigned. [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/get-started#oauth).    - Scopes: `ips:read_write`   **CLI**  `shell linode-cli networking ip-update \   203.0.113.1 \   --rdns "test.example.org" \   --reserved true `  [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/cloud-computing/docs/getting-started-with-the-linode-cli)
- [Assign IPv4s to Linodes](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/post-assign-ipv4s.md): This operation is equivalent to [Assign IP addresses](/reference/post-assign-ips).  Assign multiple IPv4 addresses and/or IPv6 ranges to multiple Linodes in one Region. This allows swapping, shuffling, or otherwise reorganizing IPs to your Linodes.  The following restrictions apply:  - All Linodes involved must have at least one public IPv4 address after assignment.   - For Linode interfaces, the Linode needs to have a public interface, and the address it receives can't be a private IPv4 address. - Linodes may have no more than one assigned private IPv4 address. - Linodes may have no more than one assigned IPv6 range.  [Open a support ticket](/reference/post-ticket) to request additional IPv4 addresses or IPv6 ranges beyond standard account limits.  > 📘 > > Removing an IP address that has been set as a Managed Linode's `ssh.ip` causes the Managed Linode's SSH access settings to reset to their default values.  To view and configure Managed Linode SSH settings, use the following operations: - [Get a Linode's managed settings](/reference/get-managed-linode-setting) - [Update a Linode's managed settings](/reference/put-managed-linode-setting)   **Permissions and scopes**  To call this operation, you need the following:  - **OAuth scopes**. Your user needs these scopes assigned. [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/get-started#oauth).    - Scopes: `ips:read_write, linodes:read_write`
- [Configure IPv4 sharing](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/post-share-ipv4s.md): This operation is equivalent to [Share IP addresses](/reference/post-share-ips).  Configure shared IPs.  IP sharing allows IP address reassignment (also referred to as IP failover) from one Linode to another if the primary Linode becomes unresponsive. This means that requests to the primary Linode's IP address can be automatically rerouted to secondary Linodes at the configured shared IP addresses.  IP failover requires configuration of a [BGP based failover service](https://techdocs.akamai.com/cloud-computing/docs/configure-failover-on-a-compute-instance) within the internal system of the primary Linode.  > 📘 > > A public IPv4 address can't be shared if it's configured for a 1:1 NAT on a legacy configuration profile VPC interface or on a Linode VPC interface.   **Permissions and scopes**  To call this operation, you need the following:  - **OAuth scopes**. Your user needs these scopes assigned. [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/get-started#oauth).    - Scopes: `ips:read_write, linodes:read_write`
- [List IPv6 pools](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/get-ipv6-pools.md): Displays the IPv6 pools on your Account. A pool of IPv6 addresses are routed to all of your Linodes in a single [region](/reference/get-regions). Any Linode on your Account may bring up any address in this pool at any time, with no external configuration required.   **Permissions and scopes**  To call this operation, you need the following:  - **OAuth scopes**. Your user needs these scopes assigned. [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/get-started#oauth).    - Scopes: `ips:read_only`   **CLI**  `shell linode-cli networking v6-pools `  [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/cloud-computing/docs/getting-started-with-the-linode-cli)
- [Create an IPv6 range](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/post-ipv6-range.md): Creates an IPv6 Range and assigns it based on the provided Linode or route target IPv6 SLAAC address. See the `ipv6` property when running the [Get a Linode](/reference/get-linode-instance) operation to view a Linode's IPv6 SLAAC address.  - Either `linode_id` or `route_target` is required in a request. - `linode_id` and `route_target` are mutually exclusive. Submitting values for both properties in a request results in an error. - Upon a successful request, an IPv6 range is created in the [region](/reference/get-regions) that corresponds to the provided `linode_id` or `route_target`. - Your Linode is responsible for routing individual addresses in the range, or handling traffic for all the addresses in the range. - Run the [Assign IP addresses](/reference/post-assign-ips) operation to re-assign IPv6 Ranges to your Linodes.  > 📘 > > - A Linode can only have one IPv6 range targeting its SLAAC address. > - An account can only have one IPv6 range in each [region](/reference/get-regions). > - [Open a support ticket](/reference/post-ticket) to request expansion of these restrictions.   **Permissions and scopes**  To call this operation, you need the following:  - **OAuth scopes**. Your user needs these scopes assigned. [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/get-started#oauth).    - Scopes: `ips:read_write, linodes:read_write`   **CLI**  `shell linode-cli networking v6-range-create \   --linode_id 123 \   --prefix_length 64 `  [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/cloud-computing/docs/getting-started-with-the-linode-cli)
- [List IPv6 ranges](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/get-ipv6-ranges.md): Displays the IPv6 ranges on your Account.    - An IPv6 range is a `/64` or `/56` block of IPv6 addresses routed to a single Linode in a given [region](/reference/get-regions).    - Your Linode is responsible for routing individual addresses in the range, or handling traffic for all the addresses in the range.    - Run the [Create an IPv6 range](/reference/post-ipv6-range) operation to add a `/64` or `/56` block of IPv6 addresses to your account.   **Permissions and scopes**  To call this operation, you need the following:  - **OAuth scopes**. Your user needs these scopes assigned. [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/get-started#oauth).    - Scopes: `ips:read_only`   **CLI**  `shell linode-cli networking v6-ranges `  [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/cloud-computing/docs/getting-started-with-the-linode-cli)
- [Get an IPv6 range](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/get-ipv6-range.md): View IPv6 range information.   **Permissions and scopes**  To call this operation, you need the following:  - **OAuth scopes**. Your user needs these scopes assigned. [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/get-started#oauth).    - Scopes: `ips:read`   **CLI**  `shell linode-cli networking v6-range-view 2001:0db8:: `  [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/cloud-computing/docs/getting-started-with-the-linode-cli)
- [Delete an IPv6 range](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/delete-ipv6-range.md): Removes this IPv6 range from your account and disconnects the range from any assigned Linodes.  > 📘 > > You can't delete shared IPv6 ranges. Contact Customer Support for assistance.   **Permissions and scopes**  To call this operation, you need the following:  - **OAuth scopes**. Your user needs these scopes assigned. [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/get-started#oauth).    - Scopes: `ips:read_write`   **CLI**  `shell linode-cli networking v6-range-delete 2001:0db8:: `  [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/cloud-computing/docs/getting-started-with-the-linode-cli)
- [Reserve a public IPv4 address](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/post-reserved-ip.md): Reserve a public IPv4 address within a specific region for future assignment to a resource. Because Reserved IPs are tied to a specific datacenter's network infrastructure, they can't be moved across regions. However, they provide a persistent public entry point for your cloud infrastructure inside that region, independent of the underlying resource. Unlike ephemeral IPs that are recycled upon deletion, a reserved IP remains in your account until manually released.  Benefits: - Rebuild or migrate Linodes and NodeBalancers without changing your public-facing IP. - Eliminates the need to update allowlists, firewall rules, automation scripts, or DNS records during deployments. - Maintain ownership of the address during failovers or maintenance windows when it may be temporarily unassigned. - Billed at a flat monthly rate per IP, regardless of assignment status. Unreserving an IP stops billing immediately and returns the address to the general pool, where it can't be recovered.   **Permissions and scopes**  To call this operation, you need the following:  - **OAuth scopes**. Your user needs these scopes assigned. [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/get-started#oauth).    - Scopes: `ips:read_write`   **CLI**  `shell linode-cli networking reserved-ip-add \   --region us-east \   --tags NB1,Linode1 `  [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/cloud-computing/docs/getting-started-with-the-linode-cli)
- [List reserved IP addresses](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/get-reserved-ips.md): Returns a paginated list of reserved IP addresses on your account.   **Permissions and scopes**  To call this operation, you need the following:  - **OAuth scopes**. Your user needs these scopes assigned. [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/get-started#oauth).    - Scopes: `ips:read_only`   **CLI**  `shell linode-cli networking reserved-ips-list `  [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/cloud-computing/docs/getting-started-with-the-linode-cli)
- [Get reserved IP pricing](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/get-reserved-ip-types.md): Returns pricing information for reserved IPs.   **Permissions and scopes**  To call this operation, you need the following:  - **OAuth scopes**. Your user needs these scopes assigned. [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/get-started#oauth).    - Scopes: `ips:read_only`   **CLI**  `shell linode-cli networking reserved-ip-types-list `  [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/cloud-computing/docs/getting-started-with-the-linode-cli)
- [Get a reserved IP address](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/get-reserved-ip.md): Returns information about a reserved IP address on your account.   **Permissions and scopes**  To call this operation, you need the following:  - **OAuth scopes**. Your user needs these scopes assigned. [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/get-started#oauth).    - Scopes: `ips:read_only`   **CLI**  `shell linode-cli networking reserved-ip-view 192.107.143.141 `  [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/cloud-computing/docs/getting-started-with-the-linode-cli)
- [Update reserved IP address tags](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/put-reserved-ip.md): Replaces the current tags for the reserved IP address with the new set provided.   **Permissions and scopes**  To call this operation, you need the following:  - **OAuth scopes**. Your user needs these scopes assigned. [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/get-started#oauth).    - Scopes: `ips:read_write`   **CLI**  `shell linode-cli networking reserved-ip-update \   192.107.143.141 \   --tags NB2, Linode2 `  [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/cloud-computing/docs/getting-started-with-the-linode-cli)
- [Unreserve an IP address](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/delete-reserved-ip.md): Permanently removes the reserved IP address from your account and stops all associated charges. Once unreserved, the IP address can't be recovered.   **Permissions and scopes**  To call this operation, you need the following:  - **OAuth scopes**. Your user needs these scopes assigned. [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/get-started#oauth).    - Scopes: `ips:read_write`   **CLI**  `shell linode-cli networking reserved-ip-delete \   192.107.143.141 `  [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/cloud-computing/docs/getting-started-with-the-linode-cli)
- [List VLANs](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/get-vlans.md): Returns a list of all Virtual Local Area Networks (VLANs) on your account. VLANs provide a mechanism for secure communication between two or more Linodes that are assigned to the same VLAN, and are both within the same Layer 2 broadcast domain.  For legacy configuration profile interfaces, you can use the following operations to create, attach, detach, and delete VLANs on a Linode: - [Create a Linode](/reference/post-linode-instance) - [Create a config profile](/reference/post-add-linode-config) - [Update a config profile](/reference/put-linode-config) - [Update](/reference/put-linode-config) the active Configuration Profile to remove the VLAN Interface, then [reboot](/reference/post-reboot-linode-instance) the Linode. - [Create a config profile](/reference/post-add-linode-config) without the VLAN Interface, then [reboot](/reference/post-reboot-linode-instance) the Linode into the new Configuration Profile. - [Delete](/reference/delete-linode-instance) the Linode.  For Linode interfaces, you can use the following operations to create, attach, detach, and delete VLANs on a Linode: - [Create a Linode](/reference/post-linode-instance) - [Add a Linode interface](/reference/post-linode-interface) - [Update a Linode interface](put-linode-interface-settings) - [Delete a Linode interface](/reference/delete-linode-interface) from a Linode.  > 📘 > > - Only Next Generation Network (NGN) data centers support VLANs. Run the [List regions](/reference/get-regions) operation to view the capabilities of data center regions. If a VLAN is attached to your Linode and you attempt to migrate or clone it to a non-NGN data center, the migration or cloning won't initiate. If a Linode cannot be migrated because of an incompatibility, you will be prompted to select a different data center or contact support. > > - See the [VLANs Overview](https://www.linode.com/docs/products/networking/vlans/#technical-specifications) to view additional specifications and limitations.   **Permissions and scopes**  To call this operation, you need the following:  - **OAuth scopes**. Your user needs these scopes assigned. [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/get-started#oauth).    - Scopes: `linodes:read_only`   **CLI**  `shell linode-cli vlans list `  [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/cloud-computing/docs/getting-started-with-the-linode-cli)
- [Delete a VLAN](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/delete-vlan.md): This operation deletes a legacy configuration profile VLAN interface. To delete a Linode interface VLAN, use the [Delete a Linode interface](/reference/delete-linode-interface) operation.  You can't delete a VLAN if it's still attached to a Linode. There are a few ways to detach it: - [Update](/reference/put-linode-config) the active configuration profile to remove the VLAN interface, then [reboot](/reference/post-reboot-linode-instance) the Linode. - [Create a config profile](/reference/post-add-linode-config) without the VLAN interface, then [reboot](/reference/post-reboot-linode-instance) the Linode into the new configuration profile. - [Delete](/reference/delete-linode-instance) the Linode.  A successful request triggers a `vlan_delete` event.  > 📘 > > VLANs without any attached Linodes are periodically cleaned up by the system.   **Permissions and scopes**  To call this operation, you need the following:  - **OAuth scopes**. Your user needs these scopes assigned. [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/get-started#oauth).    - Scopes: `linodes:read_write`   **CLI**  `shell linode-cli vlans delete $regionId $label `  [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/cloud-computing/docs/getting-started-with-the-linode-cli)

## API Reference: NodeBalancers

- [NodeBalancers index](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/nodebalancers/llms.txt): full category index
- [Create a NodeBalancer](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/post-node-balancer.md): Creates a NodeBalancer in the requested Region. Only available in [regions](/reference/get-regions) with "NodeBalancers" in their `capabilities`.  NodeBalancers require a port config with at least one backend node to start serving requests.  When using the Linode CLI to create a NodeBalancer, first create a NodeBalancer without any configs. Then, create configs and nodes for that NodeBalancer with the respective [Create a config](/reference/post-node-balancer-config) and [Create a node](/reference/post-node-balancer-node) operations.  After creating a NodeBalancer, account administrators can add a lock to prevent accidental NodeBalancer deletion by using the [Create a resource lock](/reference/post-resource-lock) operation.   **Permissions and scopes**  To call this operation, you need the following:  - **Identity and access permissions**. Your user needs a role with these permissions assigned. [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/cloud-computing/docs/identity-access-cm-available-roles).   - Permissions: `create_nodebalancer` - **OAuth scopes**. Your user needs these scopes assigned. [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/get-started#oauth).    - Scopes: `nodebalancers:read_write`   **CLI**  `shell linode-cli nodebalancers create \   --region us-east \   --label balancer12345 \   --ipv4 "192.0.2.141" \   --client_conn_throttle 0 `  [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/cloud-computing/docs/getting-started-with-the-linode-cli)
- [List NodeBalancers](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/get-node-balancers.md): Returns a paginated list of NodeBalancers you have access to.   **Permissions and scopes**  To call this operation, you need the following:  - **Identity and access permissions**. Your user needs a role with these permissions assigned. [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/cloud-computing/docs/identity-access-cm-available-roles).   - Permissions: `view_nodebalancer` - **OAuth scopes**. Your user needs these scopes assigned. [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/get-started#oauth).    - Scopes: `nodebalancers:read_only`   **CLI**  `shell linode-cli nodebalancers list `  [Learn more](https://techdocs.akamai.com/cloud-computing/docs/getting-started-with-the-linode-cli)
- [Get a NodeBalancer](https://techdocs.akamai.com/linode-api/reference/get-node-balancer.md): Returns a single NodeBalancer you can access. Run the [List VPC configurations](/reference/get-node-balancer-vpcs) operation to get the NodeBalancer's VPC configuration.   **Permissions and scopes**  To call this operation, you need the following:  - **Identity and access permissions**. Your user needs a role with these permissions assigned.
